## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nA 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. Chronic Inflammatory Demyelinating Polyneuropathy (CIDP)\nB. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)\nC. Diabetic Neuropathy\nD. Diabetic Myelopathy\n\n## Answer\n", 'response': "The patient's symptoms—including progressive gait ataxia, limb weakness, intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals—are classic for **Myasthenia Gravis (MG)**, a neuromuscular junction disorder characterized by fatigable muscle weakness.  \n\nKey points:  \n- **Fatigue-induced weakness** (worsening after meals, with fatigue) is a hallmark of MG.  \n- **Ocular symptoms** (double vision) are common in MG.  \n- **Muscle weakness** that worsens with activity and improves with rest is typical.  \n- While imaging was pending to rule out demyelinating or structural lesions, MG is not a demyelinating disease but a neuromuscular junction disorder.  \n\nThe correct diagnosis is **Myasthenia Gravis**, but it is not listed among the options. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.\n\n## Answer\nThe humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.\n\nAnswer: [C]\n\n## Question\nWhich one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?\n    \nA. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nExits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?\n\nA. 52\nB. 51\nC. 50\nD. 49\n\n## Answer\nTo maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \\(8 \times 6 = 48\\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \\(100 - 48 = 52\\) km.\n\nAnswer: [A]\n\n## Question\nA 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.\n\nAnswer: [C]\n\n## Question\nThe two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:\nA. actin and troponin.\nB. actin and myosin.\nC. troponin and tropomyosin.\nD. myosin and tropomyosin.\n\n## Answer\nActin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nA man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? \n\nA. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.\nB. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.\nC. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.\nD. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.\n\n## Answer\nIn this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nWhich one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?\n    \nA. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nA 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. prompt ###############################3PROMPT!!!!!!!!!!!!!!! ## Question
A psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?

A. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.
B. Behavior results from conditioning.
C. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.
D. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.

## Answer
The humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.

Answer: [C]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer
Actin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.

Answer: [B]

## Question
A man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? 

A. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.
B. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.
C. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.
D. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.

## Answer
In this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

## Question
Exits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?

A. 52
B. 51
C. 50
D. 49

## Answer
To maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \(8 	imes 6 = 48\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \(100 - 48 = 52\) km.

Answer: [A]

The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nA man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? \n\nA. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.\nB. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.\nC. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.\nD. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.\n\n## Answer\nIn this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nWhich one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?\n    \nA. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nA 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nA man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? \n\nA. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.\nB. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.\nC. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.\nD. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.\n\n## Answer\nIn this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nWhich one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?\n    \nA. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nA 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. Laboratory results show type 2 diabetes with stable glycemic control (no recent abnormal labs reported). Imaging revealed no prior imaging; MRI of brain and spine is pending to rule out demyelinating or structural lesions. What is the most likely final diagnosis for this patient?\n\nA. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)\nB. Chronic Inflammatory Demyelinating Polyneuropathy (CIDP)\nC. Diabetic Neuropathy\nD. Diabetic Myelopathy\n\n## Answer\n", 'response': 'The patient has progressive gait ataxia, limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, intermittent double vision, mild intermittent foot tingling, urinary urgency, slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.\nB. Behavior results from conditioning.\nC. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.\nD. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.\n\n## Answer\nThe humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nThe two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:\nA. actin and troponin.\nB. actin and myosin.\nC. troponin and tropomyosin.\nD. myosin and tropomyosin.\n\n## Answer\nActin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. prompt ###############################3PROMPT!!!!!!!!!!!!!!! ## Question
A man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? 

A. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.
B. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.
C. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.
D. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.

## Answer
In this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Exits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?

A. 52
B. 51
C. 50
D. 49

## Answer
To maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \(8 	imes 6 = 48\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \(100 - 48 = 52\) km.

Answer: [A]

## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer
Actin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.

Answer: [B]

## Question
A psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?

A. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.
B. Behavior results from conditioning.
C. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.
D. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.

## Answer
The humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.

Answer: [C]

## Question
A 62-year-old woman presents with 3 months of progressive gait ataxia and limb weakness that worsens with fatigue and improves with rest, associated with intermittent double vision, mild intermittent tingling in the feet, urinary urgency, occasional slurred speech when tired, and worsening fatigue after meals. Laboratory results show type 2 diabetes with stable glycemic control (no recent abnormal labs reported). Imaging revealed no prior imaging; MRI of brain and spine is pending to rule out demyelinating or structural lesions. What is the most likely final diagnosis for this patient?

A. Diabetic Myelopathy
B. Chronic Inflammatory Demyelinating Polyneuropathy (CIDP)
C. Diabetic Neuropathy
D. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)

## Answer

TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nExits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50.
